inside stage1_search, which runs from offset 345 to 1169. Resume preserves the
work done but not the phase schedule, which restarts at t=0 every chunk, so no
number of chunks advances the clock.

Measuring B-13 end to end needs one process living past 2307 seconds. This
environment caps foreground at the 600-second tool timeout and collects
background processes shortly after a turn ends. Three techniques were tried and
measured rather than assumed; the foreground one survives as genuinely useful
for any run up to about 600 seconds.

attempts, all of which are state built across a long run. A cold call has none

===== 50c5d05dd92562d1c7897a813b4435d1a2d7d63d
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-18T22:43:10+00:00

Deterministic A/B: remove the solver noise that undermined every comparison

CP-SAT is a portfolio solver; with several workers it races strategies and
shares information between them, so results depend on thread timing. The engine
runs two workers, and every measurement in this review inherited that. The
resulting noise band of one to four intervals forced multi-seed repeats and
produced at least two conclusions that later reversed.

Verified here that --num-workers 1 is reproducible: two runs of AE_IT_B2B at
seed 9000 returned byte-identical metrics including
after_avoidable_overage_fte_sum at 23.625. One run per arm is therefore enough,
because nothing but the code varies.

It settled B-13 immediately. Deterministic control against the B-13 tree is
identical on all six decision metrics, so the earlier four-interval target loss
that prompted the revert was noise. The revert remains correct for the reason
the arithmetic already gave: where min_after_floor is passed it is tighter than
the gameable bound, 88 against 85, so replacing it is a no-op. Now measured
rather than only argued.

tools/deterministic_ab.sh runs the pair and diffs the full canonical surface.
It is a measurement tool, not a production setting: production should keep
multiple workers because the portfolio finds better schedules in the same wall
clock.

The multi-seed repeats cost about six hours and left B-13 ambiguous. The
deterministic pair cost ten minutes and settled it.

Capture the CP-SAT telemetry the engine was discarding

All three solver call sites set log_search_progress=False and their diagnostic
dicts keep four numbers, which is why every performance question about this
engine has been unanswerable. Two specific losses:

best_objective_bound was recorded only on OPTIMAL/FEASIBLE, so it was dropped
on UNKNOWN -- exactly when it says the most about how far the search got, and
exactly the value a stop-at-the-bound rule needs. Verified against ortools
9.15.6755 that the bound is populated and meaningful on UNKNOWN.

deterministic_time was never read. It measures search effort independently of
machine load, unlike wall_time, which is the confound behind every ambiguous
comparison in this review on a contended box.

The change is observation-only: every captured value is a field the solve
already filled in, so reading it costs no solver time. The applier sets no
solver parameter and deliberately does NOT enable log_search_progress, since
logging costs wall clock and would change results on a budgeted solve. The
existing best_objective_bound key keeps its semantics because solve_breaks
consumes it for exception_lower_bound; new data lands under solver_telemetry.

Five tests, 0.076s. Mutation-checked: reverting the bound capture to the old
OPTIMAL/FEASIBLE-only form fails test_the_bound_is_captured_on_unknown.

Verified time_limit and cp_model are in scope at all three patched sites by AST
rather than assumption.

it, and editing the engine under a live experiment would contaminate it.

===== d391c9702067e61c0c987205a489936d51b1854a
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-19T18:41:46+00:00

Add the two native CP-SAT limits the engine never set, with inert defaults

max_memory_in_mb defaults to 10000 MB in ortools 9.15.6755 and the engine set
it nowhere, so the solver was working toward a 10 GB ceiling inside a cgroup
that killed it at 7.76 GB. relative_gap_limit was likewise unset, and telemetry
caught a Stage-1 solve sitting 0.00058% from proven optimal while consuming its
whole 45-second slice.

Both are termination limits, not search strategy, so neither perturbs the
subsolver portfolio the CP-SAT primer warns about. Defaults are inert: gap 0.0
is CP-SAT's own default and the memory limit is unset, so shipping this changes
no schedule until a value is chosen and A/B'd.

Six tests. Non-vacuity covered: a loose gap limit must return no later than
proving optimality and must not beat the proven optimum. One test asserts the
helper's executable code never touches worker counts or subsolver selection,
checked via AST with the docstring stripped, since the docstring legitimately
discusses both.

Research also recorded a larger unmeasured lever: CP-SAT only adds a
first-solution subsolver at 5 workers, and the shipped default resolves to 4 on
a 4-core host. That matches the UNKNOWN symptom exactly. Flagged as a testable
hypothesis, and as a caveat on every --num-workers 1 measurement in this review.

 evidence/WORKER_THRESHOLD_MEASURED.md | 63 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 63 insertions(+)
===== 0123f7fb2a9004780e78dfacefd670f729a3ca12
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-19T19:09:03+00:00

Localise the 8 GB: memory doubles AFTER the search, during finalization

A single AE_AR_B2B run at 1800s peaks at 8,244 MB. This corrects my earlier
suggestion that concurrency drove the 3600s OOM -- one run reaches 8.2 GB on
its own, corroborated by the kernel's OOM record of anon-rss 7,758,648 kB for a
single process.

The curve has two growth regions. Solver phases are flat at ~795 MB through
stage1_search and break_search. joint_refinement adds ~2.3 GB. Then memory more
than doubles, 3968 -> 8244 MB, in the final ~100 seconds spanning
target_lock_recovery and finalization -- after the search is finished.

This materially qualifies the max_memory_in_mb change just applied: that
parameter bounds CP-SAT, so if the larger share is Python work during
finalization it will not prevent this OOM. The limit is still worth having,
since CP-SAT's 10 GB default exceeds container budgets and joint_refinement is
real solver growth, but it must not be reported as the fix.

Attribution of the final 4.3 GB needs tracemalloc around finalization, not a
10-second sampler; deferred because the worker-threshold test currently owns
the machine.

 evidence/JOINT_REFINEMENT_NEVER_IMPROVES.md | 26 +++++++++++++++++++++++---

===== 8182b74a809b6b994e4b8d20917c8c3c4b5c2ae0
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-20T03:02:19+00:00

Apply the solver limits: memory ceiling 6000 MB, gap limit left off

Gate PASS at 21 suites.

MEMORY CEILING 6000 MB, chosen from measurement rather than convention:
  ~820 MB  normal peak with joint refinement off -> never binds
  ~8200 MB peak with joint refinement on         -> binds first
  ~7760 MB is where the cgroup OOM killer struck
Binding means CP-SAT returns its incumbent instead of the process being
SIGKILLed and the whole run lost. A test pins the ceiling below 7760.

GAP LIMIT LEFT AT 0.0 (off), deliberately against the industry default.
CPLEX and Gurobi both default relative MIP gap to 1e-4, and the guarantee is
that the solution lies within epsilon of optimal IN OBJECTIVE SPACE. That
guarantee does not transfer to this model: B-5 measured the objective at
400,000,000:1 across 14 tiers with 13 of them non-separating, so epsilon in
objective space does not bound coverage loss. Concretely, 1e-4 of the measured
26,211,272 objective is 2,621 absolute, which is 327 units of the weight-8
tier. Choosing a value needs an A/B against coverage, not a borrowed default.

Voice floor gap root-caused: fixed requests make RC9.1's 264 provably impossible

gives a weak bound, which gives no pruning, which gives enumeration, which
consumes the whole slice every time.

Overnight fact 3: OOM fixed at the crashing budget, and 4 workers hit the ceiling

AE_AR_B2B at 3600s -- the run the cgroup OOM killer terminated at 7,758,648 kB
with no schedule produced -- now completes at 827 MB peak, rc 0, production
eligible. A 9.4x reduction at the shipped QUICK budget; the crash class is
closed.

At 4 workers both test cases reach the theoretical ceiling at 1800s:
AE_AR_B2B 168/168 on 168 active intervals and AE_FR_B2B 112/112 on 112, i.e.
100% target coverage before and after breaks with zero break loss. AE_AR_B2B
at 168/168 matches the RC9.1 production baseline exactly.

This reframes the budget question. The 1-worker curve was still climbing at
1800s (131 -> 142 -> 165), which is why I concluded long budgets were genuinely
needed and retracted the earlier oversized claim. At 4 workers the same case is
finished at 1800s, and 600s@4w matches 1800s@1w. That conclusion was an
artifact of a crippled configuration and the retraction was probably the wrong
call.

Scoped deliberately: two workbooks, both of which may simply be capacity-rich.
The hard cases (Cricut_Chat, Cricut_Voice, NMG, GDI) have not been run at 4
workers, and no 3600s@4w run exists, so 'QUICK is 2x oversized' holds for these
two cases and is not yet a basis for moving the default.

two capacity-rich cases hitting 100% at 1800s. The hard cases contradict it:
GDI climbs +9 and Chat +5 from 1800 to 3600. The corpus splits into two
populations and a single budget cannot serve both, so 3600 stays.

FACT 2 retracts the gap-stop lever I proposed and built. Across 167 telemetry
records the median relative gap is 88.93% and zero of 103 full-slice solves
would stop at any threshold from 1e-6 to 1e-3. The 0.00058% solve I generalised
from was an outlier. The replacement reading is better supported: the engine is
bound-starved rather than time-starved, which redirects to B-5 tested for speed.

FACT 3 confirms the OOM fix at the exact budget that crashed: 7760 MB SIGKILL
with no schedule becomes 827 MB and a valid production-eligible result.

FACT 1 counts the strategy portfolio: 6 of 15 have never executed, 4 more never
win, and 2 of 7 break modes never execute.

B-5 tested for speed: compressed weights turn UNKNOWN into FEASIBLE

B-5 was closed for coverage impact and never tested for solve behaviour. Same
workbook, profile, seed and 120s slice, with profile weights remapped
rank-preserving so tier order is unchanged and only the spread narrows:
AE_AR_B2B and Cricut_Chat both return UNKNOWN on the original weights and
FEASIBLE on the compressed ones.

On Cricut_Chat the search changes character: conflicts 11 -> 381 and branches
per conflict 369,110 -> 7,139. That is the mechanism the bound-starvation
reading predicted -- poor spread degrades the relaxation, the bound stays weak,
nothing prunes, the search enumerates.

Scoped honestly: two cases, one profile, one slice length, and the compression
reached only the four profile-level weights spanning 91:1, not the
400,000,000:1 range that arises during model construction. Neither run reached
the metrics stage so coverage was not compared, and since B-5 established the
tiers do not separate, compression can change which solution is optimal.

Relevance: this is the first evidence that objective conditioning affects
whether a solution is found at all, giving the unexplained Stage-2 UNKNOWN
results a candidate mechanism that has not been ruled out.

===== 9b3d0534b47fd0a0aac76f87d542eaf211440783
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-20T16:54:26+00:00

Explain the Cricut Chat worker regression: Stage-1 is blind to break compatibility

More workers produce a better skeleton and a worse schedule on this workbook.
It is neither noise nor a selection bug.

At 4 workers Stage-1 learns ~10,000x more conflicts per profile (201k-438k
against 0-36 at one worker) and branches-per-conflict falls from 73,797 to
1.4-7.1, so the search is working as intended. The candidate pool shows why the
result gets worse: every high-coverage skeleton loses about 60 intervals to
break placement (194->130, 193->134, 190->132, 187->129) while the weakest one
loses 10 (169->159). The selector ranks on after_target and correctly picks
159, the best finished schedule available.

The defect is upstream: Stage-1 optimises before-break coverage with no model of
break compatibility, so a skeleton that scores better on its own objective can
be strictly worse as a finished schedule. At one worker the search was too weak
to find those tightly-packed skeletons and never hit the trap.

This is the mechanism behind what was previously recorded as a symptom -- the
breakability trade-off in A34 and Gate 5 "rewards a worse skeleton" -- and it
raises the stakes on joint refinement, the phase built to optimise shifts and
breaks together, now measured at 69 attempts across 7 workbooks with zero
improvements.

would solve a problem that does not exist. The earlier reasoning confused a

Stage-2: pre-register the decisive test before running it

NIGHT_06 proved after_target 159 is reachable on the skeleton production
delivered at 130. Reachability does not name the defect. obj(159) vs
obj(130) does, because min_target_hits only adds a constraint (l632:7800)
while the objective is assembled identically and minimised at :7823 --
same function, different feasible region.

Sized the penalty pools from the workbook first. The quality terms are
large enough to cover 29 target intervals (140 of 224 run windows), so
"the objective honestly prefers 130" is not absurd. But all_gap is forced
only when all 4 intervals of a window are missed, so tripping 140 windows
needs ~143 consecutive floor misses and drags a ~357M floor_miss bill with
it. That makes the weighting verdict require a 1:4.8 target-to-floor
exchange rate on thresholds over the same coverage counts.

Prediction recorded before the probe runs: SEARCH DEFECT.

Root cause: Stage-2's break search plans 168 attempts and runs zero

C_CHAT_1800_w4 records STOPPED_INSUFFICIENT_BREAK_SEARCH_DEPTH with
attempts_completed 0 of attempts_planned 168. break_search is allocated
452s; 175s were already gone when the phase began (absolute deadline
offsets mean a Stage-1 overrun consumes break_search rather than delaying
it), the guaranteed anchor took 135s and returned UNKNOWN/rejected, and
the 139.851s left fell under the 180s floor.

So four of six production skeletons entered the pool only as
fully_compliant_diagnostic_fallback -- minimum_exception_diagnostic
output, which is blind to coverage because the quality block is gated
behind `if not diagnostic_mode`. Their 58-64 interval "break damage" is
not break damage; it is a coverage-blind model being read as a break
result.

The 3600s run is the natural experiment: same workbook, same skeleton at
before_target 194, diagnostic fallback gives 137 and a real 180s
target_priority solve gives 164. Across its three real attempts the
objective is monotone in coverage (164 -> 1.96e9, 135 -> 3.54e9,
131 -> 3.91e9).

That answers NIGHT_08's pre-registered question against my own earlier
suspicion: the objective ranks coverage correctly. The search is starved.

Census over 117 audits: at the production QUICK budget of 1800s, 15 of 22
runs place breaks without ever running the break search.

Measurement only. Two fix candidates recorded, neither applied -- one
recovered attempt is not automatically an improvement, and the budget
ladder change needs a per-workbook A/B first.

   tests_staged/ -- written and mutation-checked earlier -- never executed.
   Wired them in behind RC9_ENGINE_DIR so they target the candidate tree
   rather than the production engine, with a guard that refuses to credit a
   suite reporting "Ran 0 tests" and one that refuses to run at all if the
   candidate tree is missing. Gate is now 16 suites, and verified to exit 1
   in both new failure modes.

- preference_kind("Annual Leave") was pinned to "other" per C-3. LEAVE_WORDS

  It has none. Re-pinned CLEAN and added a round-trip test proving a canonical
  surface survives re-canonicalization. Mutation-checked: removing the
  self-reference fails both.

===== 9b17ddeedc9b13d87ae94eb2755c4f72a90eac25
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-23T04:26:01+00:00

Stage-2: the anchor may no longer eat the last fundable break attempt

NIGHT_09 measured the defect: on Cricut Chat at 1800s the break_search
phase had 277s left, the guaranteed anchor took 135 and returned
UNKNOWN/rejected, and the 139.8s remainder fell under the 180s floor the
adaptive search refuses to start below. It ran 0 of 168 planned attempts.
Across 22 recorded 1800s runs, 15 placed breaks without ever running the
break search at all.

The floor itself is correct (B-10, retracted and re-derived: 90-150s
returns 160, 180s returns 162, so a shorter attempt ships a worse
schedule). The defect is upstream - the phase never HAS 180 seconds to
offer, because the anchor spent them.

stage2_anchor_slice_seconds now leaves BREAK_MIN_MEANINGFUL_SLICE_SEC
behind whenever the phase can pay for both. On the recorded Chat case the
anchor goes 135 -> 97 and the remainder becomes exactly 180: one real
attempt instead of none. At 3600s, where the phase already funded three
attempts, the grant is unchanged at 240. Below anchor-minimum plus the
break floor it is inert and the old allowance stands.

Five tests, mutation-checked: deleting the cap fails them, and the engine
was restored byte-identical afterwards. They pin the recorded Chat case,
the untouched roomy case, that the cap never pushes the anchor under its
own minimum, that it is inert when the phase cannot pay for both, and
that the grant never exceeds the phase.

Gate: PASS, 16 suites. A/B against the unchanged engine on AE_AR_B2B at
1800s/1 worker/seed 9000 is running; this is reverted if it regresses.

===== 258efc4a9900f98f508da0b79b3efc1ae97758cf
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-23T04:30:46+00:00

W7/B-9: independently recompute the language_reserve gate, and settle the
target-lock arithmetic

language_reserve was the second of the three release gates with no
independent check. The validator already re-derived the HARD minimum
(eligible < rule.minimum -> language_gaps); nothing re-derived the
operational RESERVE the gate actually scores, which is
minimum + language_reserve_extra.

Added that recomputation next to the existing per-quarter language check,
so it reuses the same after-break eligible count rather than a second
traversal, and calls the engine's own language_operational_reserve_target
and language_reserve_status instead of reimplementing the tiers, which
could drift. Emits language_reserve_independent with the three tier counts,
the gate mode and up to twenty worked examples.

Ten tests, mutation-checked twice. The first version of the after-break
test passed under mutation because it searched text near the call and
'after[slot]' was still on a nearby line; it now walks the AST, requires
the first argument to language_reserve_status to be a NAME, and requires
that name to be bound from after[slot] and never from before[slot].
Scoring the reserve before breaks, by either a len() or an inline
comprehension, now fails it. The validator was restored byte-identical
after each mutation.

Also settled the target-lock question NIGHT_08 left open. The model
constrains ceil_units(req * ratio) * qpi and the metric compares
after_pct >= ratio, two different arithmetics. Measured across all 242
demanded intervals of the Cricut Chat workbook: the model threshold is
STRICTER on 242 and looser on 0, so min_target_hits=N does force
after_target >= N. NIGHT_06's proof therefore stands and the broad
retraction in NIGHT_08 was itself too broad. Pinned as a test over the
packaged AE_AR_B2B demand.

The gate now runs the staged suites against the merged engine/ rather than
the pre-merge snapshot, which is what shipping code should be tested
against; candidates/ stays as provenance.

Gate: PASS, 16 suites.

Measure non-monotonicity instead of asserting it

Raw corpus: 287 of 1469 same-workbook budget pairs show the larger budget
scoring lower. That number is confounded -- the pairs mix worker counts,
seeds and engine builds -- and is not the effect size.

Controlled (one session, one build, 4 workers, same workbook at 1800 and
3600): Chat +5, GDI +9, Voice -1. Two of three improve clearly; one moves
by a single interval, which is the size of the multi-worker noise already
measured here. So non-monotonicity is NOT established beyond noise, and
proving it would need repeats per budget that have not been run.

===== 199219b3e8315c3ba4529e8bd577975d76d1b988
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-23T04:38:00+00:00

Close the gate hole the merge fell through: check cross-module call signatures

The merge shipped a caller from one lineage and its callee from another --
build_global_budget_plan(..., diagnostics=...) against a definition with no
such parameter. Every suite passed and both selfchecks passed, because
neither enters run_case, and ruff's F821 cannot see it: the NAME resolves,
only the keyword was wrong. The defect surfaced only when a real 1800s run
died on its first call.

tools/check_cross_module_calls.py resolves each module's imports of its
local siblings, finds the target definitions, and checks every call site's
keywords and positional count against the real signature. Unexpected
keywords, too many positionals, and missing required or keyword-only args
are all reported with file and line.

Verified non-vacuous against the actual bug: deleting the diagnostics
parameter again makes it print

  l632_universal_scheduler.py:18279: build_global_budget_plan() ->
  phase_b_maturity.build_global_budget_plan(): unexpected keyword 'diagnostics'

and exit 1. The file was restored byte-identical afterwards. It skips call
sites with **kwargs or *args splats rather than guessing.

Wired into run_tests.sh next to the undefined-name sweep. It runs in under a
second, so the cheapest possible guard against the most expensive class of
defect this project has hit.

===== d99a641517a25512f6d3c86668919b80292e2030
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-23T16:33:07+00:00

Four fixes found by the synthetic suite, each proven on the case that exposed it

1. Hard-feasibility probe retries UNKNOWN before giving up. SYNTH_X1 and
   the Union Airways benchmark (247 agents) stopped FAIL_HARD_CONTRACT_UNKNOWN
   after a 30s probe with >1,100s unspent. The retry borrows half the Stage-1
   window (cap 600s); Union N=247 now yields a valid schedule, validator and
   parity PASS.

2. Budget-starved Stage-1 runs one before-basis profile. When the portfolio
   cannot fund its first profile, the only skeleton came from the productive
   proxy, which assumes break loss is spread evenly and stacked 11 on some
   days, 7 on others (SYNTH_E2: 45/63). The first before-basis profile now
   runs on the leftover seconds: E2 at 300s is 63/63 before and after,
   status PASS. Inert whenever any portfolio profile ran.

3. Break-capacity advisory uses the metric's interval averaging. Counting
   room per quarter against ceil(requirement) told a 60-minute, 14-person
   roster meeting target in 63/63 intervals with zero break losses to hire
   one more. Room is now floor(sum of quarter heads - qpi x requirement),
   each quarter still capped by concurrency; identical at 15 minutes.

4. Joint search stops on low memory instead of being OOM-killed. SYNTH_X1
   reached the no-candidate joint endgame and was SIGKILLed at 13.9 GB with
   no audit. Both joint loops now check MemAvailable (>= max(2 GB, 15%))
   before building a model and record the stop.

Tests: tests_staged/test_rc9_2_14 (10) and test_rc9_2_15 (13); 8 of the
latter fail on the previous engine. Gate PASS, 22 suites.

===== 3bfda692a8ebb1bc4b55dd4982ef3e73344246a3
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-23T18:07:11+00:00

Blank-interval staffing counts current-week staffing only; suite fixtures; S2-PAR held back

FA-7 on the final engine: NMG SP failed engine/validator metric parity on
blank_staffed_quarters, 16 vs 0. The engine counted last Saturday's carry-in
over blank (no-demand) quarters as staffing, under a comment claiming the
validator did the same; the validator counts current-week staffing, which
is what "no new staffing in blank intervals" means. The mismatch is already
in the RC5 candidate. Carry-in covers 16 blank quarters in that workbook;
an empty week now reports 0. Pinned by a test that fails on the previous
engine.

fixtures/synthetic_suite/ holds the 14 synthetic and 4 benchmark workbooks,
their certified expectations, and the independent exact solutions.

patches/S2_PAR_stage2_parallel_break_solve.patch is NOT applied. With the
break-infeasibility core on (default), every Stage-2 solve runs on one
worker. The patch solves a clone on all workers and extracts the core only
on INFEASIBLE. Component A/B (120s, 4 workers): Chat 165 -> 167; AE_AR,
H1 and H3 go from no solution to 166/113/92; M2 100 -> 96. The rule written
before the run allowed no case worse by more than 2, so it is held back.

Gate PASS, 22 suites.

===== 37288d0b9edd526924df976b73e3fe105adbf3b8

Stage-2 break solves use every worker (S2-PAR), shipped on its end-to-end A/B

With the break-infeasibility core on (default), every break model carried
assumption literals and so every Stage-2 solve ran single-worker, whatever
--num-workers said; single-worker CP-SAT runs no LNS and could not improve
on its warm start. With more than one worker the solve now runs on a clone
with the family literals fixed true; only an INFEASIBLE result is re-solved
with assumptions (one worker, <= 60s) to name the core. One worker: path
unchanged.

Pre-registered end-to-end A/B (900s, 2 workers, seed 9000, production
runner): Cricut Chat 155 -> 179, Cricut Voice 227 -> 246, AE_AR_B2B
166 -> 167, NMG_SP 121 = 121, SYNTH_M2 82 -> 109 (optimum 117), SYNTH_H3

PASS; no workbook worse. Rule and scores in evidence/s2par_e2e/.

===== 0762e0f07dbc389a156938bd4e3f48b9814be1fa
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-24T21:52:31+00:00

Seed portfolio runner: run K seeds, keep best validated after- and before-breaks

engine/RUN_PORTFOLIO.py wraps the production runner. Each seed is a complete,
untouched run; the after-breaks winner must pass independent validation with 0
hard failures and parity PASS; the before-breaks sheet is chosen separately,
so neither is worse than any of its own seed runs. Opt-in (--seeds, default 1);
mode defaults change only on a pre-registered measurement.

Evidence (retrospective, small n): same engine, same input, 900 s vs 3600 s
leaves real-workbook means unchanged while seeds spread Chat 167-182.

===== 2f2a9ce0ff4cb119a66480850b581c3666b5c0c2
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-25T20:18:07+00:00

Joint-solve isolation: forked, memory-watched joint-refinement solves (verification pending)

Each CP-SAT solve in joint refinement runs in a fork of the engine (same
model object, parameters and seed); the parent parses the child's response,
so Value/ObjectiveValue/StatusName are unchanged. On memory exhaustion only
the child dies (watchdog below max(256 MB, 2% RAM) free; oom_score_adj 1000),
the solve reports UNKNOWN, the audit records it and no further joint models
start. Non-memory child errors are re-raised. In-process fallback without
fork or with callbacks.

Verified so far (evidence/joint_solve_isolation): bit-identical results on
deterministic solves; the Chat DEEP replay that died with MemoryError now
returns normally; gate PASS (26 suites). Still running under the registered
rule: real-model equivalence on M2/H1 and a 3600 s DEEP end-to-end run.
RELEASE_IDENTITY and the Colab package are unchanged until the rule passes;
the commit is reverted if it fails.

===== 16ce1570915d7318daf3247ee642743588373976
Author: Claude
Date:   2026-09-30T14:27:33+00:00

Validator: cross-check the parsed contract against the raw workbook

F-13: the independent validator took the input contract from the
engine's own parse_input, so a parser defect was invisible to parity.
independent_input_crosscheck re-reads the workbook with openpyxl alone:
roster names, demand hours per time bucket against a Sun-Sat sheet, and
(when enabled) Preference/Fixed cell by cell - a parsed value needs a
workbook cell behind it, and a leave/OFF cell must not parse as blank.
A mismatch is a hard failure; an unrecognised layout is a visible
NOT_CHECKED warning, never a pass.

Corpus: 50 workbooks, 0 mismatches, 0 not-checked; validating real
schedules gives PASS with metrics identical to the recorded ones.
engine/tools/VALIDATOR_INDEPENDENCE.md documents the engine surface the
validator still shares. Five injected parser defects each fail;
each comparison removed in turn is caught by the tests.

Gate: 48 suites, 1236 tests, 2 skipped.
